static r_no_return void stop_unsupported_extremum(
  const char* op,
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* y,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct rray_arg* y_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  r_abort_lazy_call(
    error_call,
    "Can't apply `%s` to %s and %s.",
    op,
    rray_arg_type_format(x_arg, rray_typeof(x)),
    rray_arg_type_format(y_arg, rray_typeof(y))
  );
}

#define RRAY_EXTREMUM(                                                         \
  X_CTYPE,                                                                     \
  X_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
  X_CAST,                                                                      \
  Y_CTYPE,                                                                     \
  Y_CONST_DEREF,                                                               \
  Y_CAST,                                                                      \
  OUT_RTYPE,                                                                   \
  OUT_CTYPE,                                                                   \
  OUT_DEREF,                                                                   \
  ONE                                                                          \
)                                                                              \
  r_obj* out = KEEP(r_alloc_vector(OUT_RTYPE, size));                          \
  OUT_CTYPE* v_out = OUT_DEREF(out);                                           \
                                                                               \
  const X_CTYPE* v_x = X_CONST_DEREF(x);                                       \
  const Y_CTYPE* v_y = Y_CONST_DEREF(y);                                       \
                                                                               \
  RRAY_ITERATOR2_FOR_EACH(it, i, x_loc, y_loc, {                               \
    v_out[i] = ONE(X_CAST(v_x[x_loc]), Y_CAST(v_y[y_loc]), na_rm);             \
  });                                                                          \
                                                                               \
  FREE(1);                                                                     \
  return out;

#undef RRAY_EXTREMUM

static inline int rray_pmax_int_one(int x, int y, bool na_rm) {
  if (x == r_globals.na_int) {
    return na_rm ? y : x;
  }
  if (y == r_globals.na_int) {
    return na_rm ? x : y;
  }
  return x < y ? y : x;
}

static inline double rray_pmax_dbl_one(double x, double y, bool na_rm) {
  if (ISNAN(x)) {
    if (ISNAN(y)) {
      return y;
    }
    return na_rm ? y : x;
  }
  if (ISNAN(y)) {
    return na_rm ? x : y;
  }
  return x < y ? y : x;
}

77 more lines cut

The helper currently has three decisions per element: two NA checks and the max comparison; na_rm also influences each NA branch. I’m checking what the compiler already turns into conditional moves, because source-level ternaries don’t necessarily mean runtime branches.

static r_obj* rray_extremum(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* y,
  bool na_rm,
  enum rray_extremum_op op,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct rray_arg* y_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  check_unclassed(x, x_arg, error_call);
  check_unclassed(y, y_arg, error_call);

  x = KEEP(arg_as_array(x, x_arg, error_call));
  y = KEEP(arg_as_array(y, y_arg, error_call));

  r_obj* x_dimensions = r_dim(x);
  r_obj* y_dimensions = r_dim(y);

  const int* v_x_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(x_dimensions);
  const int* v_y_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(y_dimensions);

  const int x_dimensionality =
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(x_dimensions);
  const int y_dimensionality =
    rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(y_dimensions);

  r_obj* dimensions = KEEP(rray_dimensions2(
    v_x_dimensions,
    x_dimensionality,
    v_y_dimensions,
    y_dimensionality,
    x_arg,
    y_arg,
    error_call
  ));
  const int* v_dimensions = r_int_cbegin(dimensions);
  const int dimensionality = rray_dimensionality_from_dimensions(dimensions);

  const r_ssize size = rray_size_from_dimensions(v_dimensions, dimensionality);

  struct rray_iterator2 it;
  rray_iterator2_init(
    &it,
    size,
    v_dimensions,
    dimensionality,
    v_x_dimensions,
    x_dimensionality,
    v_y_dimensions,
    y_dimensionality
  );

  r_obj* out = KEEP(
    rray_extremum_switch(x, y, op, size, &it, na_rm, x_arg, y_arg, error_call)
  );
  r_attrib_poke_dim(out, dimensions);

  r_obj* out_names = KEEP(rray_broadcast_names2(x, y, dimensions));

  if (out_names != r_null) {
    r_attrib_poke_dim_names(out, out_names);
  }

  FREE(5);
  return out;
}

static r_obj* rray_extremum_switch(
  r_obj* x,
  r_obj* y,
  enum rray_extremum_op op,
  r_ssize size,
  struct rray_iterator2* it,
  bool na_rm,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct rray_arg* y_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  const enum rray_type x_type = rray_typeof(x);
  const enum rray_type y_type = rray_typeof(y);

  enum rray_side side;

  switch (rray_typeof2(x_type, y_type, &side)) {
  case RRAY_TYPE2_logical_logical:
    switch (op) {
    case RRAY_EXTREMUM_max:
      return rray_pmax_lgl_lgl(x, y, size, it, na_rm, error_call);
    case RRAY_EXTREMUM_min:
      return rray_pmin_lgl_lgl(x, y, size, it, na_rm, error_call);
    }
  case RRAY_TYPE2_logical_integer:
    switch (op) {
    case RRAY_EXTREMUM_max:
      if (side == RRAY_SIDE_right) {
        return rray_pmax_lgl_int(x, y, size, it, na_rm, error_call);
      } else {
        return rray_pmax_int_lgl(x, y, size, it, na_rm, error_call);
      }
    case RRAY_EXTREMUM_min:
      if (side == RRAY_SIDE_right) {
test_that("computes parallel maxima and minima", {

#ifndef RRAY_ITERATOR_H
#define RRAY_ITERATOR_H

#include "dimensionality.h"
#include "rlang.h"

#include "decl/iterator-decl.h"

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------

// Simplest iterator
//
// Walks the multidimensional point space, providing access to the current
// multidimensional point
struct rray_point_iterator {
  r_ssize index;
  r_ssize size;

  int v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  int v_point_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  int point_dimensionality;
};

static inline void rray_point_iterator_init(
  struct rray_point_iterator* it,
  r_ssize size,
  const int* v_point_dimensions,
  int point_dimensionality
) {
  check_max_dimensionality(point_dimensionality);

  it->index = 0;
  it->size = size;

  it->point_dimensionality = point_dimensionality;

  for (int i = 0; i < point_dimensionality; ++i) {
    it->v_point_dimensions[i] = v_point_dimensions[i];
  }
  memset(it->v_point, 0, sizeof(int) * point_dimensionality);
}

#define RRAY_POINT_ITERATOR_FOR_EACH(IT, INDEX, V_POINT, ...)                  \
  do {                                                                         \
    struct rray_point_iterator* const iterator = (IT);                         \
    r_ssize INDEX = iterator->index;                                           \
    const r_ssize size = iterator->size;                                       \
    int* V_POINT = iterator->v_point;                                          \
    const int* v_point_dimensions = iterator->v_point_dimensions;              \
    const int point_dimensionality = iterator->point_dimensionality;           \
                                                                               \
    while (INDEX != size) {                                                    \
      __VA_ARGS__                                                              \
      ++INDEX;                                                                 \
                                                                               \
      for (int axis = 0; axis < point_dimensionality; ++axis) {                \
        ++V_POINT[axis];                                                       \
                                                                               \
        if (V_POINT[axis] < v_point_dimensions[axis]) {                        \
          break;                                                               \
        }                                                                      \
                                                                               \
        V_POINT[axis] = 0;                                                     \
      }                                                                        \
    }                                                                          \
  } while (0)

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------

// Broadcasting iterator
//
// Walks the multidimensional point space. Reports a 1D `location` in an
// alternate subspace.
//
// For broadcasting, the dimensions you broadcast to make up the larger point
// space. This is walked in order. The original dimensions of the array make
// up the subspace. So as you walk the output's point space you can fetch
// `location`s back into your original array to pull from.
//
// For reducing, it's actually a special form of broadcasting. The original
// dimensions of the array are the point space. The reduced dimensions are the
// subspace. So as you walk the original array, you can fetch `location`s into
// the output to accumulate the reduced result at.
struct rray_iterator {
  r_ssize index;
  r_ssize size;

  // Since coalescing can multiply two axes' dimensions together, we use an
  // `r_ssize` here even though an individual dimension can't be above an `int`.
  r_ssize v_point[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  r_ssize v_point_dimensions[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
  int point_dimensionality;

  r_ssize location;
  r_ssize v_location_strides[RRAY_MAX_DIMENSIONALITY];
};

static inline void rray_iterator_init(
  struct rray_iterator* it,
  r_ssize size,
  const int* v_point_dimensions,
  int point_dimensionality,
  const int* v_location_dimensions,
  int location_dimensionality
) {
  check_max_dimensionality(point_dimensionality);

  it->index = 0;
  it->size = size;

  for (int i = 0; i < point_dimensionality; ++i) {
    it->v_point_dimensions[i] = (r_ssize) v_point_dimensions[i];
  }
  memset(it->v_point, 0, sizeof(r_ssize) * point_dimensionality);

  rray__location_strides_init(
    it->v_location_strides,
    v_point_dimensions,
    point_dimensionality,
    v_location_dimensions,
    location_dimensionality,
    "location"
  );
  it->location = 0;

  it->point_dimensionality = rray__iterator_axes_coalesce(
    it->v_point_dimensions,
    it->v_location_strides,
    point_dimensionality
  );
}

// For-loop-style iteration, giving access to the flat index and mapped
// location.
//
// The loop structure is more complicated than strictly necessary for
// performance reasons. We process a run along the first axis while holding all
// other axes fixed, so the inner loop only advances the index and location by
// fixed increments. Point adjustment happens between runs. This makes the
// inner loop easier for the compiler to optimize and vectorize. In our
// benchmarks, batching rows improved performance by up to 3-4× and helped our
// operations outperform base R in many cases, even with broadcasting support.
#define RRAY_ITERATOR_FOR_EACH(IT, INDEX, LOCATION, ...)                       \
  do {                                                                         \
    /* Unpacking the iterator fields is critical for performance. It gives */  \
    /* the compiler guarantees about loop counters and fixed inputs that */    \
    /* unlock vectorization optimizations. */                                  \
    struct rray_iterator* const iterator = (IT);                               \
    r_ssize INDEX = iterator->index;                                           \
    const r_ssize size = iterator->size;                                       \
    r_ssize* v_point = iterator->v_point;                                      \
    const r_ssize* v_point_dimensions = iterator->v_point_dimensions;          \
    const int point_dimensionality = iterator->point_dimensionality;           \
    r_ssize LOCATION = iterator->location;                                     \
    const r_ssize* v_location_strides = iterator->v_location_strides;          \
                                                                               \
    const r_ssize rows = v_point_dimensions[0];                                \
    const r_ssize row_stride = v_location_strides[0];                          \
    const r_ssize row_reset = rows * row_stride;                               \
                                                                               \
    while (INDEX != size) {                                                    \
      /* Apply expression for each row */                                      \
      for (r_ssize row = 0; row < rows; ++row) {                               \
        __VA_ARGS__                                                            \
        LOCATION += row_stride;                                                \
        ++INDEX;                                                               \
      }                                                                        \
      LOCATION -= row_reset;                                                   \
                                                                               \
      /* We've iterated over the rows of this batch. */                        \
      /* Advance to the start of the next batch of rows. */                    \
      for (int axis = 1; axis < point_dimensionality; ++axis) {                \
        ++v_point[axis];                                                       \
                                                                               \
        if (v_point[axis] < v_point_dimensions[axis]) {                        \
          LOCATION += v_location_strides[axis];                                \
          break;                                                               \
        }                                                                      \
                                                                               \
        v_point[axis] = 0;                                                     \
                                                                               \
        LOCATION -= (v_point_dimensions[axis] - 1) * v_location_strides[axis]; \
      }                                                                        \
    }                                                                          \
  } while (0)

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------

// walking the point space once

  r_ssize index;
  r_ssize size;

  // Since coalescing can multiply two axes' dimensions together, we use an
  // `r_ssize` here even though an individual dimension can't be above an `int`.

#define RRAY_ITERATOR2_FOR_EACH(IT, INDEX, LOCATION1, LOCATION2, ...)          \
  do {                                                                         \
    struct rray_iterator2* const iterator = (IT);                              \
    r_ssize INDEX = iterator->index;                                           \
    const r_ssize size = iterator->size;                                       \
    r_ssize* v_point = iterator->v_point;                                      \
    const r_ssize* v_point_dimensions = iterator->v_point_dimensions;          \

    r_ssize LOCATION1 = iterator->location1;                                   \
    const r_ssize* v_location1_strides = iterator->v_location1_strides;        \
    r_ssize LOCATION2 = iterator->location2;                                   \
    const r_ssize* v_location2_strides = iterator->v_location2_strides;        \
                                                                               \
    const r_ssize rows = v_point_dimensions[0];                                \
    const r_ssize row_stride1 = v_location1_strides[0];                        \
    const r_ssize row_stride2 = v_location2_strides[0];                        \
    const r_ssize row_reset1 = rows * row_stride1;                             \
    const r_ssize row_reset2 = rows * row_stride2;                             \
                                                                               \
    while (INDEX != size) {                                                    \
      for (r_ssize row = 0; row < rows; ++row) {                               \
        __VA_ARGS__                                                            \
        LOCATION1 += row_stride1;                                              \
        LOCATION2 += row_stride2;                                              \
        ++INDEX;                                                               \
      }                                                                        \
      LOCATION1 -= row_reset1;                                                 \
      LOCATION2 -= row_reset2;                                                 \
                                                                               \
      for (int axis = 1; axis < point_dimensionality; ++axis) {                \
        ++v_point[axis];                                                       \
                                                                               \
        if (v_point[axis] < v_point_dimensions[axis]) {                        \
          LOCATION1 += v_location1_strides[axis];                              \
          LOCATION2 += v_location2_strides[axis];                              \
          break;                                                               \
        }                                                                      \
                                                                               \
        v_point[axis] = 0;                                                     \
                                                                               \
        LOCATION1 -=                                                           \
          (v_point_dimensions[axis] - 1) * v_location1_strides[axis];          \
        LOCATION2 -=                                                           \
          (v_point_dimensions[axis] - 1) * v_location2_strides[axis];          \
      }                                                                        \
    }                                                                          \
  } while (0)

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------

static inline void rray__location_strides_init(
  r_ssize* v_location_strides,
  const int* v_point_dimensions,
  int point_dimensionality,
  const int* v_location_dimensions,
  int location_dimensionality,
  const char* location_arg
) {
  if (location_dimensionality > point_dimensionality) {
    r_stop_internal(
      "`%s_dimensionality` of %d can't be greater than "
      "`point_dimensionality` of %d.",
      location_arg,
      location_dimensionality,
      point_dimensionality
    );
  }

  for (int i = 0; i < location_dimensionality; ++i) {
    const int point_dimension = v_point_dimensions[i];
    const int location_dimension = v_location_dimensions[i];

    if (location_dimension != point_dimension && location_dimension != 1) {
      r_stop_internal(
        "Axis %d of `%s` must have a dimension of 1 or %d, not %d.",
        i + 1,
        location_arg,
        point_dimension,
        location_dimension
      );
    }
  }

  r_ssize stride = 1;
  for (int i = 0; i < point_dimensionality; ++i) {
    const int dimension =
      (i < location_dimensionality) ? v_location_dimensions[i] : 1;
    v_location_strides[i] = (dimension == 1) ? 0 : stride;
    stride *= dimension;
  }
}

// Coalescing axes is an important optimization used to reduce the number of
// axes we have to iterate over by "merging" adjacent compatible ones. This can
// 3-5x performance on its own in some cases. It's easiest to look at some
// practical applications of how this is useful:
//
// - Identically shaped binary array operations. Adding two arrays with
//   dimensions [2, 4, 5] gives both inputs strides [1, 2, 8]. All adjacent axes
//   coalesce (2 * 1 = 2, 4 * 2 = 8) into one dimension [40] with stride [1],
//   resulting in one flat vectorizable loop.
//
// - Scalar broadcasting across an entire array. Adding a scalar to a [2, 4, 5]
//   array gives the array strides [1, 2, 8] and the scalar strides [0, 0, 0].
//   Both coalesce completely, producing a [40] loop with array stride [1] and
//   scalar stride [0].
//
// - Contiguous adjacent axes within a broadcast operation. Broadcasting a
//   [2, 3] array over point dimensions [2, 3, 4] produces location strides
//   [1, 2, 0]. The adjacent axes 1 and 2 coalesce (2 * 1 = 2) into size 6,
//   leaving dimensions [6, 4] and strides [1, 0].
//
// - Contiguous regions within a reduction. Reducing a [2, 3, 4] array over its
//   third axis maps into a [2, 3] result using location strides [1, 2, 0].
//   Coalescing produces dimensions [6, 4], making it the same as the broadcast
//   example above.
//
// - Point-space dimensions of size 1. Output point dimensions of [1, 3, 4] can
//   have strides [0, 1, 3]. The dimension 1 first axis is absorbed into the
//   dimension 3 second axis, adopting stride 1, after which the dimension 4
//   third axis also coalesces. The result is one dimension [12] with stride
//   [1]. Note that this isn't broadcasting. This is when both input and output
//   have an axis that stays dimension 1, which is somewhat rare.
//
// For iterator2, note that both sets of location strides must be coalescible,
// as coalescing changes the output dimensionality, so it's all or nothing.
static inline int rray__iterator_axes_coalesce(
  r_ssize* v_dimensions,
  r_ssize* v_strides,
  int dimensionality
) {
  int out_axis = 0;

  for (int axis = 1; axis < dimensionality; ++axis) {
    const r_ssize left_dimension = v_dimensions[out_axis];

Analyzing compiler branchless optimization

Clarifying NA handling in max function

Thinking

Analyzing compiler branchless optimization

Clarifying NA handling in max function

There’s a useful semantic shortcut: R’s integer NA is INT_MIN. With na_rm = TRUE, ordinary signed max(x, y) already does exactly the right thing—INT_MIN loses to every nonmissing integer, and two NAs remain NA. That suggests specializing once outside the element loop rather than carrying full NA logic through every iteration. I’m validating the generated ARM64 code for that shape.

Edit /private/tmp/rray_pmax_codegen.c 
*** Begin Patch
*** Add File: /private/tmp/rray_pmax_codegen.c
+#include <stdbool.h>
+#include <stddef.h>
+#include <stdint.h>
+
+#define NA_INT INT32_MIN
+
+static inline int current(int x, int y, bool na_rm) {
+  if (x == NA_INT) {
+    return na_rm ? y : x;
+  }
+  if (y == NA_INT) {
+    return na_rm ? x : y;
+  }
+  return x < y ? y : x;
+}
+
+static inline int collapsed(int x, int y, bool na_rm) {
+  const int out = x < y ? y : x;
+  if (!na_rm && (x == NA_INT || y == NA_INT)) {
+    return NA_INT;
+  }
+  return out;
+}
+
+void loop_current(const int* x, const int* y, int* out, size_t n, bool na_rm) {
+  for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+    out[i] = current(x[i], y[i], na_rm);
+  }
+}
+
+void loop_collapsed(const int* x, const int* y, int* out, size_t n, bool na_rm) {
+  for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+    out[i] = collapsed(x[i], y[i], na_rm);
+  }
+}
+
+void loop_remove(const int* x, const int* y, int* out, size_t n) {
+  for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+    out[i] = x[i] < y[i] ? y[i] : x[i];
+  }
+}
+
+void loop_propagate(const int* x, const int* y, int* out, size_t n) {
+  for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+    const int xi = x[i];
+    const int yi = y[i];
+    const int max = xi < yi ? yi : xi;
+    out[i] = (xi == NA_INT || yi == NA_INT) ? NA_INT : max;
+  }
+}
*** End Patch

   719	
   720	static inline int rray_pmax_int_one(int x, int y, bool na_rm) {
   721	  if (x == r_globals.na_int) {
   722	    return na_rm ? y : x;
   723	  }
   724	  if (y == r_globals.na_int) {
   725	    return na_rm ? x : y;
   726	  }
   727	  return x < y ? y : x;
   728	}
   729	
   730	static inline double rray_pmax_dbl_one(double x, double y, bool na_rm) {
   731	  if (ISNAN(x)) {
   732	    if (ISNAN(y)) {
   733	      return y;
   734	    }
   735	    return na_rm ? y : x;
   736	  }
   737	  if (ISNAN(y)) {
   738	    return na_rm ? x : y;
   739	  }
   740	  return x < y ? y : x;
   741	}

static inline int rray_cast_lgl_to_int_one(int x) {
  return x;
}

static inline double rray_cast_lgl_to_dbl_one(int x) {
  if (x == r_globals.na_lgl) {
    return r_globals.na_dbl;
  }

  return (double) x;
}

static inline r_complex rray_cast_lgl_to_cpl_one(int x) {
  const double out = (x == r_globals.na_lgl) ? r_globals.na_dbl : (double) x;
  return (r_complex) {.r = out, .i = 0};
}

static inline double rray_cast_int_to_dbl_one(int x) {
  if (x == r_globals.na_int) {
    return r_globals.na_dbl;
  }

  return (double) x;
}

static inline r_complex rray_cast_int_to_cpl_one(int x) {
  const double out = (x == r_globals.na_int) ? r_globals.na_dbl : (double) x;
  return (r_complex) {.r = out, .i = 0};
}

static inline r_complex rray_cast_dbl_to_cpl_one(double x) {
  return (r_complex) {.r = x, .i = 0};
}

// --------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Lossy

static inline int rray_cast_int_to_lgl_one(
  int x,
  r_ssize i,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  if (x == r_globals.na_int) {
    return r_globals.na_lgl;
  }

  if (x == 0 || x == 1) {
    return x;
  }

  stop_lossy_cast(RRAY_TYPE_integer, RRAY_TYPE_logical, i, x_arg, error_call);
}

static inline int rray_cast_dbl_to_lgl_one(
  double x,
  r_ssize i,
  struct rray_arg* x_arg,
  struct r_lazy error_call
) {
  if (ISNAN(x)) {
    return r_globals.na_lgl;
  }

*** Begin Patch
*** Update File: /private/tmp/rray_pmax_codegen.c
@@
 static inline int collapsed(int x, int y, bool na_rm) {
@@
 }
+
+static inline int masked(int x, int y, bool na_rm) {
+  const int out = x < y ? y : x;
+  const bool has_na = (x == NA_INT) | (y == NA_INT);
+  const bool propagate_na = !na_rm & has_na;
+  return propagate_na ? NA_INT : out;
+}
@@
 void stride_collapsed(
@@
 }
+
+
+void stride_masked(
+  const int* x,
+  const int* y,
+  int* out,
+  size_t n,
+  size_t sx,
+  size_t sy,
+  bool na_rm
+) {
+  size_t ix = 0;
+  size_t iy = 0;
+  for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+    out[i] = masked(x[ix], y[iy], na_rm);
+    ix += sx;
+    iy += sy;
+  }
+}
*** End Patch

Edit /private/tmp/rray_pmax_codegen.c 
*** Begin Patch
*** Update File: /private/tmp/rray_pmax_codegen.c
@@
 void stride_masked(
@@
 }
+
+
+void stride_split(
+  const int* x,
+  const int* y,
+  int* out,
+  size_t n,
+  size_t sx,
+  size_t sy,
+  bool na_rm
+) {
+  size_t ix = 0;
+  size_t iy = 0;
+
+  if (na_rm) {
+    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+      const int xi = x[ix];
+      const int yi = y[iy];
+      out[i] = xi < yi ? yi : xi;
+      ix += sx;
+      iy += sy;
+    }
+  } else {
+    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
+      const int xi = x[ix];
+      const int yi = y[iy];
+      const int max = xi < yi ? yi : xi;
+      const bool has_na = (xi == NA_INT) | (yi == NA_INT);
+      out[i] = has_na ? NA_INT : max;
+      ix += sx;
+      iy += sy;
+    }
+  }
+}
*** End Patch

Thinking

Planning microbenchmark with vectorization focus

Analyzing optimized integer max helper logic

Thinking

Planning microbenchmark with vectorization focus

Analyzing optimized integer max helper logic

The result is clear: on this ARM64/Clang build, the existing helper has no data-dependent jump—the compiler emits conditional selects—but it emits about five selects per element and declines to vectorize the integer loops. A mask-based rewrite vectorizes; splitting on na_rm once gives the cleanest code.

Analyzing NA caching and aliasing issues

Yes. The current implementation at extremum.c is already compiled without data-dependent jumps on ARM64, but Clang emits roughly five conditional selects per element and does not vectorize the integer loops.

The smallest improvement is:

static inline int rray_pmax_int_one(int x, int y, bool na_rm) {
  const int na = r_globals.na_int;
  const int out = x < y ? y : x;

  const bool has_na = (x == na) | (y == na);
  const bool propagate_na = !na_rm & has_na;

  return propagate_na ? na : out;
}

Using bitwise | and & is intentional. In a standalone reproduction, Clang reduced this to two selects and vectorized the contiguous loop at width four.

For maximum performance, branch once outside the element loop:

static inline int pmax_int_remove(int x, int y) {
  return x < y ? y : x;
}

static inline int pmax_int_propagate(int x, int y, int na) {
  const int out = x < y ? y : x;
  const bool has_na = (x == na) | (y == na);
  return has_na ? na : out;
}

Then select the whole loop based on na_rm. This works because R integer NA is INT_MIN:

  • na_rm = TRUE: ordinary signed max automatically ignores one NA; two NAs still produce NA.
  • na_rm = FALSE: use the combined NA mask to propagate NA.

That produces one pre-loop branch, while the hot na_rm = TRUE path becomes a plain smax vector loop. I’d benchmark the split-loop version first; it has the strongest codegen, at the cost of duplicating the iterator body. Also cache r_globals.na_int outside the loop for the propagation path—the current generated code reloads it inside the loop.

No repository files were changed or committed.

Davis

Oh you think re loading na_int matters?

Codex

Probably only marginally. The generated loop does reload r_globals.na_int each iteration, but it will remain hot in L1 and the load can overlap with other work.

The larger costs are:

  • Five conditional selects per element.
  • The dependency chain between those selects.
  • Failure to vectorize the integer loop.

Caching na_int might gain a few percent in the na_rm = FALSE path, but I wouldn’t optimize it independently. Splitting on na_rm outside the loop matters much more: the TRUE path eliminates NA checks and the load entirely, becoming a vectorizable signed max.

Davis

Does the double implementation have similar potential?

Codex

Evaluating NaN handling logic

Optimizing NaN selection logic

Evaluating NaN handling logic

Optimizing NaN selection logic

Deriving branchless NaN handling formulas

Yes, although the upside is probably smaller because Clang already vectorizes the double loops.

The asymmetry of the existing max expression is useful:

const double out = x < y ? y : x;

A comparison involving NaN is false, so out becomes x. That lets the helper collapse to:

static inline double rray_pmax_dbl_one(
  double x,
  double y,
  bool na_rm
) {
  const double out = x < y ? y : x;
  const double missing = na_rm ? x : y;

  return ISNAN(missing) ? y : out;
}

Why it works:

  • na_rm = FALSE: out already propagates a missing x; only a missing y needs correction.
  • na_rm = TRUE: out already ignores a missing y; only a missing x needs correction.
  • If both are missing, it returns y, preserving the existing NA/NaN behavior.
  • The original tie and signed-zero behavior is preserved because the max expression is unchanged.

For best codegen, specialize outside the loop:

// na_rm = TRUE
return ISNAN(x) ? y : out;

// na_rm = FALSE
return ISNAN(y) ? y : out;

That reduces each path to one max comparison, one NaN test, and one selection. Unlike the integer rewrite, it probably improves instruction count rather than unlocking vectorization.

Davis

Edit bench/extremum.R 
*** Begin Patch
*** Add File: bench/extremum.R
+devtools::load_all(quiet = TRUE)
+
+size <- as.integer(Sys.getenv("RRAY_BENCH_SIZE", "1000000"))
+iterations <- as.integer(Sys.getenv("RRAY_BENCH_ITERATIONS", "15"))
+output <- Sys.getenv("RRAY_BENCH_OUTPUT", "")
+
+side <- as.integer(sqrt(size))
+if (side * side != size) {
+  stop("`RRAY_BENCH_SIZE` must be a perfect square.", call. = FALSE)
+}
+
+dimensions <- c(side, side)
+
+cases <- expand.grid(
+  operation = c("pmax", "pmin"),
+  type = c("integer", "double"),
+  na_rm = c(FALSE, TRUE),
+  missing = c("none", "sparse", "dense"),
+  traversal = c("contiguous", "scalar", "broadcast_axis0", "broadcast_axis1"),
+  stringsAsFactors = FALSE
+)
+
+make_values <- function(n, type, offset) {
+  values <- (seq_len(n) * (offset * 2L + 1L)) %% 2001L - 1000L
+
+  if (type == "integer") {
+    as.integer(values)
+  } else {
+    as.double(values) + offset / 10
+  }
+}
+
+add_missing <- function(x, type, missing, offset) {
+  if (missing == "none") {
+    return(x)
+  }
+
+  step <- if (missing == "sparse") 101L + offset else 2L + offset
+  locations <- seq.int(1L + offset, length(x), by = step)
+
+  if (type == "integer") {
+    x[locations] <- NA_integer_
+  } else {
+    midpoint <- length(locations) %/% 2L
+    x[locations[seq_len(midpoint)]] <- NA_real_
+    x[locations[-seq_len(midpoint)]] <- NaN
+  }
+
+  x
+}
+
+make_inputs <- function(type, missing, traversal) {
+  x <- make_values(size, type, 1L)
+
+  y_size <- switch(
+    traversal,
+    contiguous = size,
+    scalar = 1L,
+    broadcast_axis0 = side,
+    broadcast_axis1 = side
+  )
+  y <- make_values(y_size, type, 2L)
+
+  x <- add_missing(x, type, missing, 0L)
+  y <- add_missing(y, type, missing, 1L)
+
+  x <- array(x, dimensions)
+  y <- switch(
+    traversal,
+    contiguous = array(y, dimensions),
+    scalar = array(y, c(1L, 1L)),
+    broadcast_axis0 = array(y, c(1L, side)),
+    broadcast_axis1 = array(y, c(side, 1L))
+  )
+
+  list(x = x, y = y)
+}
+
+results <- vector("list", nrow(cases))
+
+for (i in seq_len(nrow(cases))) {
+  case <- cases[i, ]
+  inputs <- make_inputs(case$type, case$missing, case$traversal)
+  fn <- switch(case$operation, pmax = rray_pmax, pmin = rray_pmin)
+
+  measurement <- bench::mark(
+    extremum = fn(inputs$x, inputs$y, na_rm = case$na_rm),
+    iterations = iterations,
+    check = FALSE,
+    memory = FALSE,
+    filter_gc = FALSE
+  )
+
+  results[[i]] <- cbind(
+    case,
+    median_ns = as.numeric(measurement$median) * 1e9,
+    itr_per_sec = as.numeric(measurement$`itr/sec`)
+  )
+}
+
+results <- do.call(rbind, results)
+row.names(results) <- NULL
+
+print(results, row.names = FALSE)
+
+if (nzchar(output)) {
+  saveRDS(
+    list(
+      results = results,
+      size = size,
+      iterations = iterations,
+      revision = system2("git", c("rev-parse", "HEAD"), stdout = TRUE)
+    ),
+    output
+  )
+}
*** End Patch

+static inline int rray_pmax_int_one_propagate_na(int x, int y) {
+  const int out = x < y ? y : x;
+  const int na = r_globals.na_int;
+  const bool has_na = (x == na) | (y == na);
+
+  return has_na ? na : out;
+}
+
+static inline int rray_pmax_int_one_remove_na(int x, int y) {
+  // Since integer `NA` is `INT_MIN`, a regular maximum removes a single `NA`.
+  return x < y ? y : x;
+}
+
+static inline double rray_pmax_dbl_one_propagate_na(double x, double y) {
+  const double out = x < y ? y : x;
+
+  // A comparison involving `NaN` is false, so `out` already propagates `x`.
+  // Selecting `y` when it is missing also ensures that the second missing
+  // value wins when both inputs are missing.
+  return ISNAN(y) ? y : out;
+}
+
+static inline double rray_pmax_dbl_one_remove_na(double x, double y) {
+  const double out = x < y ? y : x;
+
+  // `out` already removes a missing `y`. A missing `x` is the only case that
+  // needs to be overridden. This also returns `y` when both inputs are missing.
+  return ISNAN(x) ? y : out;
+}
+
+static inline int rray_pmin_int_one_propagate_na(int x, int y) {
+  // Since integer `NA` is `INT_MIN`, a regular minimum propagates `NA`.
+  return x > y ? y : x;
+}
+
+static inline int rray_pmin_int_one_remove_na(int x, int y) {
+  const int na = r_globals.na_int;
+  int out = x > y ? y : x;
+
+  out = y == na ? x : out;
+  out = x == na ? y : out;
+
+  return out;
+}
+
+static inline double rray_pmin_dbl_one_propagate_na(double x, double y) {
+  const double out = x > y ? y : x;
+
+  return ISNAN(y) ? y : out;
+}
+
+static inline double rray_pmin_dbl_one_remove_na(double x, double y) {
+  const double out = x > y ? y : x;
+
+  return ISNAN(x) ? y : out;
+}
*** End Patch

* DONE (rray4)

The C implementation now has one na_rm branch per operation, outside traversal. The optimized hot paths preserve the deliberate “second missing value wins” rule for NA_real_/NaN, and I extended the same specialization to pmin because it shares the extremum machinery and has symmetric opportunities. The package compiles cleanly; next I’m stress-testing all value combinations before trusting the benchmark numbers.

+
+test_that("all native missing-value combinations match base R", {
+  integer_values <- c(NA_integer_, -1L, 0L, 1L)
+  double_values <- c(NA_real_, NaN, -Inf, -1, -0, 0, 1, Inf)
+
+  cases <- list(
+    integer = list(
+      x = rep(integer_values, each = length(integer_values)),
+      y = rep(integer_values, times = length(integer_values))
+    ),
+    double = list(
+      x = rep(double_values, each = length(double_values)),
+      y = rep(double_values, times = length(double_values))
+    ),
+    integer_double = list(
+      x = rep(integer_values, each = length(double_values)),
+      y = rep(double_values, times = length(integer_values))
+    ),
+    double_integer = list(
+      x = rep(double_values, each = length(integer_values)),
+      y = rep(integer_values, times = length(double_values))
+    )
+  )
+
+  for (case in cases) {
+    for (na_rm in c(FALSE, TRUE)) {
+      expect_identical(
+        as.vector(rray_pmax(case$x, case$y, na_rm = na_rm)),
+        pmax(case$x, case$y, na.rm = na_rm)
+      )
+      expect_identical(
+        as.vector(rray_pmin(case$x, case$y, na_rm = na_rm)),
+        pmin(case$x, case$y, na.rm = na_rm)
+      )
+    }
+  }
+})

opt <- readRDS("/private/tmp/extremum-optimized.rds")$results
keys <- c("operation", "type", "na_rm", "missing", "traversal")
x <- merge(base, opt, by = keys, suffixes = c("_before", "_after"))
x$speedup <- x$median_ns_before / x$median_ns_after
agg <- aggregate(speedup ~ operation + type + na_rm + traversal, x, median)
agg$speedup <- round(agg$speedup, 2)
print(agg, row.names = FALSE)
cat("\nOverall:\n")
print(summary(x$speedup))
cat("Cases improved:", sum(x$speedup > 1), "/", nrow(x), "\n")
cat("Integer median:", median(x$speedup[x$type == "integer"]), "\n")
cat("Double median:", median(x$speedup[x$type == "double"]), "\n")
'

Bash: Rscript -e ' 
$ Rscript -e '
base <- readRDS("/private/tmp/extremum-baseline-confirm.rds")$results
opt <- readRDS("/private/tmp/extremum-optimized-confirm.rds")$results
keys <- c("operation", "type", "na_rm", "missing", "traversal")
x <- merge(base, opt, by = keys, suffixes = c("_before", "_after"))
x$speedup <- x$median_ns_before / x$median_ns_after
agg <- aggregate(speedup ~ operation + type + na_rm + traversal, x, median)
agg$speedup <- round(agg$speedup, 2)
print(agg, row.names = FALSE)
cat("\nBy type:\n")
print(aggregate(speedup ~ type, x, function(x) round(median(x), 2)), row.names = FALSE)
cat("\nBy operation/type:\n")
print(aggregate(speedup ~ operation + type, x, function(x) round(median(x), 2)), row.names = FALSE)
cat("\nOverall:\n")
print(summary(x$speedup))
cat("Cases improved:", sum(x$speedup > 1), "/", nrow(x), "\n")
cat("Regressions:\n")
print(x[x$speedup < 1, c(keys, "speedup")], row.names = FALSE)
'

Assessing regression noise in optimization

Bash: Rscript -e ' 
$ Rscript -e '
base <- readRDS("/private/tmp/extremum-baseline-confirm.rds")$results
opt <- readRDS("/private/tmp/extremum-optimized-confirm.rds")$results
keys <- c("operation", "type", "na_rm", "missing", "traversal")
x <- merge(base, opt, by = keys, suffixes = c("_before", "_after"))
group <- c("operation", "type", "na_rm", "traversal")
b <- aggregate(x$median_ns_before, x[group], median); names(b)[5] <- "before_ms"
a <- aggregate(x$median_ns_after, x[group], median); names(a)[5] <- "after_ms"
s <- aggregate(x$median_ns_before / x$median_ns_after, x[group], median); names(s)[5] <- "speedup"
out <- Reduce(function(x, y) merge(x, y, by = group), list(b, a, s))
out$before_ms <- round(out$before_ms / 1e6, 3)
out$after_ms <- round(out$after_ms / 1e6, 3)
out$speedup <- round(out$speedup, 2)
write.table(out, row.names = FALSE, sep = " | ", quote = FALSE)
'

Verifying test and benchmark inclusion

The confirmation run used 25 iterations per cell over 1,000,000-element outputs. Median speedup across the full grid is 3.00× for integer and 1.54× for double; contiguous integer cases are about 3.8×, while broadcast-heavy double cases approach 2×. I’ve removed the temporary baseline worktree and am preparing the focused commit, excluding the unrelated bench/broadcast.R.

Commit: 412e383 Optimize extremum missing-value loops
Open a pull request

Changes:

  • Moved na_rm branching outside the hot iterator loop.
  • Added specialized NA-propagating/removing integer and double helpers.
  • Optimized both pmax and pmin.
  • Added exhaustive integer/double/mixed missing-value tests.
  • Added the reproducible benchmark grid.
  • Confirmed width-4 vectorization for integer loops.

Benchmarks used 1,000,000-element outputs, 25 iterations per cell. Times below are median milliseconds across none/sparse/dense missingness.

## feature/extremum-optimize...origin/feature/extremum-optimize

benchmark_library <- Sys.getenv("RRAY_BENCH_LIBRARY", unset = NA_character_)

if (!is.na(benchmark_library)) {
  .libPaths(c(benchmark_library, .libPaths()))
}

devtools::load_all(quiet = TRUE)

if (!requireNamespace("broadcast", quietly = TRUE)) {
  stop("The broadcast package must be installed.", call. = FALSE)
}

iterations <- 30L

benchmark_pair <- function(group, case, rray4, comparison, other) {
  rray4()
  other()
  gc()

  result <- bench::mark(
    rray4 = rray4(),
    comparison = other(),
    iterations = iterations,
    check = TRUE,
    memory = TRUE
  )

  data.frame(
    group = group,
    case = case,
    implementation = c("rray4", comparison),
    median_ms = as.numeric(result$median) * 1000,
    mem_alloc_mb = as.numeric(result$mem_alloc) / 1024^2,
    relative_time = as.numeric(result$median) / min(as.numeric(result$median)),
    row.names = NULL
  )
}

bind_results <- function(...) {
  do.call(rbind, list(...))
}

n <- 1e6L
x <- array(seq(1, 2, length.out = n), c(1000L, 1000L))
y <- array(seq(2, 3, length.out = n), c(1000L, 1000L))
scalar <- array(2, c(1L, 1L))
row <- array(seq(2, 3, length.out = 1000L), c(1L, 1000L))
column <- array(seq(2, 3, length.out = 1000L), c(1000L, 1L))
alternating_x <- array(
  seq(1, 2, length.out = 1000L),
  c(1L, 10L, 1L, 10L, 1L, 10L)
)
alternating_y <- array(
  seq(2, 3, length.out = 1000L),
  c(10L, 1L, 10L, 1L, 10L, 1L)
)

addition <- bind_results(
  benchmark_pair(
    "add",
    "same shape",
    \() rray_add(x, y),
    "broadcast",
    \() broadcast::bc.d(x, y, "+")
  ),
  benchmark_pair(
    "add",
    "scalar",
    \() rray_add(x, scalar),
    "broadcast",
    \() broadcast::bc.d(x, scalar, "+")
  ),
  benchmark_pair(
    "add",
    "row",
    \() rray_add(x, row),
    "broadcast",
    \() broadcast::bc.d(x, row, "+")
  ),
  benchmark_pair(
    "add",
    "column",
    \() rray_add(x, column),
    "broadcast",
    \() broadcast::bc.d(x, column, "+")
  ),
  benchmark_pair(
    "add",
    "alternating 6d",
    \() rray_add(alternating_x, alternating_y),
    "broadcast",
    \() broadcast::bc.d(alternating_x, alternating_y, "+")
  )
)

broadcasting <- bind_results(
  benchmark_pair(
    "broadcast",
    "scalar to matrix",
    \() rray_broadcast(scalar, c(1000L, 1000L)),
    "broadcast",
    \() broadcast::rep_dim(scalar, c(1000L, 1000L))
  ),
  benchmark_pair(
    "broadcast",
    "row to matrix",
    \() rray_broadcast(row, c(1000L, 1000L)),
    "broadcast",
    \() broadcast::rep_dim(row, c(1000L, 1000L))
  ),
  benchmark_pair(
    "broadcast",
    "column to matrix",
    \() rray_broadcast(column, c(1000L, 1000L)),
    "broadcast",
    \() broadcast::rep_dim(column, c(1000L, 1000L))
  ),
  benchmark_pair(
    "broadcast",
    "alternating 6d",
    \() rray_broadcast(alternating_x, rep(10L, 6L)),
    "broadcast",
    \() broadcast::rep_dim(alternating_x, rep(10L, 6L))
  )
)

high_dimensional <- array(seq(1, 2, length.out = n), rep(10L, 6L))

reductions <- bind_results(
  benchmark_pair(
    "sum",
    "matrix axis 1",
    \() rray_sum(x, 1L),
    "base",
    \() array(colSums(x), c(1L, 1000L))
  ),
  benchmark_pair(
    "sum",
    "matrix axis 2",
    \() rray_sum(x, 2L),
    "base",
    \() array(rowSums(x), c(1000L, 1L))
  ),
  benchmark_pair(
    "sum",
    "matrix both axes",
    \() rray_sum(x, c(1L, 2L)),
    "base",
    \() array(sum(x), c(1L, 1L))
  ),
  benchmark_pair(
    "sum",
    "6d odd axes",
    \() rray_sum(high_dimensional, c(1L, 3L, 5L)),
    "base",
    \() {
      array(
        apply(high_dimensional, c(2L, 4L, 6L), sum),
        c(1L, 10L, 1L, 10L, 1L, 10L)
      )
    }
  ),
  benchmark_pair(
    "sum",
    "6d even axes",
    \() rray_sum(high_dimensional, c(2L, 4L, 6L)),
    "base",
    \() {
      array(
        apply(high_dimensional, c(1L, 3L, 5L), sum),
        c(10L, 1L, 10L, 1L, 10L, 1L)
      )
    }
  )
)

results <- bind_results(addition, broadcasting, reductions)
results$median_ms <- round(results$median_ms, 3L)
results$mem_alloc_mb <- round(results$mem_alloc_mb, 3L)
results$relative_time <- round(results$relative_time, 2L)

print(results, row.names = FALSE)
cat("\nR:", as.character(getRversion()), "\n")
cat("rray4:", as.character(utils::packageVersion("rray4")), "\n")
cat("broadcast:", as.character(utils::packageVersion("broadcast")), "\n")
cat("platform:", R.version$platform, "\n")

   759	  const double out = x < y ? y : x;
   760	
   761	  // A comparison involving `NaN` is false, so `out` already propagates `x`.
   762	  // Selecting `y` when it is missing also ensures that the second missing
   763	  // value wins when both inputs are missing.
   764	  return ISNAN(y) ? y : out;
   765	}

   771	  // needs to be overridden. This also returns `y` when both inputs are missing.
   772	  return ISNAN(x) ? y : out;

   783	
   784	  out = y == na ? x : out;

   826	// - `x = NA`, `y = NA`: returns `NA` (`out = NA`; both replacements are NA).

   828	  const int na = r_globals.na_int;
   829	  int out = x > y ? y : x;
   830	
   831	  out = y == na ? x : out;
   832	  out = x == na ? y : out;
   833	
   834	  return out;
   835	}
   836	
   837	// As with `pmax`, a comparison involving a missing double is false, so `out`
   838	// selects `x`. Testing `y` implements propagation and second-missing-wins:
   839	// - `x = 1`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(y) = false`).
   840	// - `x = 1`, `y = NaN`: returns `NaN` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   841	// - `x = 1`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `NA_real_`
   842	//   (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   843	// - `x = NaN`, `y = 1`: returns `NaN` (`out = NaN`, `ISNAN(y) = false`).
   844	// - `x = NaN`, `y = NaN`: returns `y` (`out = x`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   845	// - `x = NaN`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `NA_real_`
   846	//   (`out = NaN`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   847	// - `x = NA_real_`, `y = 1`: returns `NA_real_`
   848	//   (`out = NA_real_`, `ISNAN(y) = false`).
   849	// - `x = NA_real_`, `y = NaN`: returns `NaN`
   850	//   (`out = NA_real_`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   851	// - `x = NA_real_`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `y`
   852	//   (`out = x`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   853	static inline double rray_pmin_dbl_one_propagate_na(double x, double y) {
   854	  const double out = x > y ? y : x;
   855	
   856	  return ISNAN(y) ? y : out;
   857	}
   858	
   859	// As with `pmax`, a comparison involving a missing double is false, so `out`
   860	// selects `x`. Testing `x` implements removal and second-missing-wins:
   861	// - `x = 1`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(x) = false`).
   862	// - `x = 1`, `y = NaN`: returns `1` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(x) = false`).
   863	// - `x = 1`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `1`
   864	//   (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(x) = false`).
   865	// - `x = NaN`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = NaN`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).
   866	// - `x = NaN`, `y = NaN`: returns `y` (`out = x`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).
   867	// - `x = NaN`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `NA_real_`
   868	//   (`out = NaN`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).

   874	//   (`out = x`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).

   767	// A comparison involving a missing double is false, so `out` selects `x`.
   768	// Testing `y` then propagates a single missing value and makes the second
   769	// missing value win when both inputs are missing:
   770	// - `x = 1`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(y) = false`).
   771	// - `x = 1`, `y = NaN`: returns `NaN` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).

   785	// A comparison involving a missing double is false, so `out` selects `x`.
   786	// Testing `x` then replaces it with `y` exactly when that is required:
   787	// - `x = 1`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(x) = false`).
   788	// - `x = 1`, `y = NaN`: returns `1` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(x) = false`).
   789	// - `x = 1`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `1` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(x) = false`).

   792	// - `x = NaN`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `NA_real_` (`out = NaN`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).
   793	// - `x = NA_real_`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = NA_real_`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).
   794	// - `x = NA_real_`, `y = NaN`: returns `NaN` (`out = NA_real_`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).

   796	static inline double rray_pmax_dbl_one_remove_na(double x, double y) {

   803	// - `x = 1`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = 1`).

   806	// - `x = NA`, `y = NA`: returns `NA` (`out = NA`).
   807	static inline int rray_pmin_int_one_propagate_na(int x, int y) {
   808	  return x > y ? y : x;
   809	}
   810	
   811	// A regular minimum selects `NA`, so each missing operand is replaced by the
   812	// other operand:
   813	// - `x = 1`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = 1`; neither replacement applies).
   814	// - `x = 1`, `y = NA`: returns `1` (`out = NA`; replace `y` with `x`).
   815	// - `x = NA`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = NA`; replace `x` with `y`).
   816	// - `x = NA`, `y = NA`: returns `NA` (`out = NA`; both replacements are NA).
   817	static inline int rray_pmin_int_one_remove_na(int x, int y) {
   818	  const int na = r_globals.na_int;
   819	  int out = x > y ? y : x;
   820	
   821	  out = y == na ? x : out;
   822	  out = x == na ? y : out;
   823	
   824	  return out;
   825	}
   826	
   827	// As with `pmax`, a comparison involving a missing double is false, so `out`
   828	// selects `x`. Testing `y` implements propagation and second-missing-wins:
   829	// - `x = 1`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(y) = false`).
   830	// - `x = 1`, `y = NaN`: returns `NaN` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   831	// - `x = 1`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `NA_real_` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   832	// - `x = NaN`, `y = 1`: returns `NaN` (`out = NaN`, `ISNAN(y) = false`).
   833	// - `x = NaN`, `y = NaN`: returns `y` (`out = x`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   834	// - `x = NaN`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `NA_real_` (`out = NaN`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   835	// - `x = NA_real_`, `y = 1`: returns `NA_real_` (`out = NA_real_`, `ISNAN(y) = false`).
   836	// - `x = NA_real_`, `y = NaN`: returns `NaN` (`out = NA_real_`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   837	// - `x = NA_real_`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `y` (`out = x`, `ISNAN(y) = true`).
   838	static inline double rray_pmin_dbl_one_propagate_na(double x, double y) {
   839	  const double out = x > y ? y : x;
   840	
   841	  return ISNAN(y) ? y : out;
   842	}
   843	
   844	// As with `pmax`, a comparison involving a missing double is false, so `out`
   845	// selects `x`. Testing `x` implements removal and second-missing-wins:
   846	// - `x = 1`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(x) = false`).
   847	// - `x = 1`, `y = NaN`: returns `1` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(x) = false`).
   848	// - `x = 1`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `1` (`out = 1`, `ISNAN(x) = false`).
   849	// - `x = NaN`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = NaN`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).
   850	// - `x = NaN`, `y = NaN`: returns `y` (`out = x`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).
   851	// - `x = NaN`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `NA_real_` (`out = NaN`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).
   852	// - `x = NA_real_`, `y = 1`: returns `1` (`out = NA_real_`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).
   853	// - `x = NA_real_`, `y = NaN`: returns `NaN` (`out = NA_real_`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).
   854	// - `x = NA_real_`, `y = NA_real_`: returns `y` (`out = x`, `ISNAN(x) = true`).
   855	static inline double rray_pmin_dbl_one_remove_na(double x, double y) {
   856	  const double out = x > y ? y : x;
   857	
   858	  return ISNAN(x) ? y : out;
   859	}

Generating detailed benchmark comment with summary tables

Clang 17 confirms 36 vectorized hot loops: both na_rm specializations for all 18 generated pmax/pmin type-pair functions. Strictly, the *_one_* helpers are scalar inline functions; after inlining, every helper use has a vectorized loop version. Double/double uses width 2, and all other pairings use width 4. Runtime still selects a scalar fallback for traversal shapes that fail the vector loop’s stride/alias guards.

Edit /private/tmp/pr46-extremum-benchmarks.md 
*** Begin Patch
*** Add File: /private/tmp/pr46-extremum-benchmarks.md
+## Extremum optimization benchmarks
+
+Before/after results for the `na_rm` loop specialization in `412e383`.
+
+### Method
+
+- Baseline: `efa64a7` (`main` before this PR)
+- Optimized implementation: `412e383`
+- Platform: ARM64 macOS, Apple Clang 17, R 4.6.0 alpha
+- Output size: 1,000,000 elements
+- Iterations: 25 per cell
+- Full grid: operation × storage type × `na_rm` × missingness × traversal = 96 cells
+- Missingness regimes: none, sparse, and dense
+- Times below are median milliseconds across the three missingness regimes; speedup is the median per-cell before/after ratio
+
+### `pmax`
+
+| Traversal | Integer, `na_rm = FALSE` | Integer, `na_rm = TRUE` | Double, `na_rm = FALSE` | Double, `na_rm = TRUE` |
+|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
+| Contiguous | 1.145 → 0.304 ms (**3.77×**) | 1.143 → 0.301 ms (**3.78×**) | 0.788 → 0.698 ms (**1.13×**) | 0.753 → 0.705 ms (**1.08×**) |
+| Scalar | 1.113 → 0.661 ms (**1.69×**) | 1.097 → 0.536 ms (**2.08×**) | 1.734 → 0.884 ms (**1.96×**) | 1.738 → 0.869 ms (**1.98×**) |
+| Broadcast axis 0 | 1.132 → 0.663 ms (**1.71×**) | 1.126 → 0.529 ms (**2.13×**) | 1.758 → 0.878 ms (**1.96×**) | 1.777 → 0.886 ms (**2.01×**) |
+| Broadcast axis 1 | 1.135 → 0.295 ms (**3.91×**) | 1.118 → 0.272 ms (**4.12×**) | 0.732 → 0.550 ms (**1.31×**) | 0.697 → 0.548 ms (**1.22×**) |
+
+### `pmin`
+
+| Traversal | Integer, `na_rm = FALSE` | Integer, `na_rm = TRUE` | Double, `na_rm = FALSE` | Double, `na_rm = TRUE` |
+|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
+| Contiguous | 1.147 → 0.296 ms (**3.86×**) | 1.150 → 0.293 ms (**3.97×**) | 0.774 → 0.689 ms (**1.12×**) | 0.756 → 0.696 ms (**1.09×**) |
+| Scalar | 1.112 → 0.521 ms (**2.13×**) | 0.986 → 0.752 ms (**1.46×**) | 1.692 → 0.887 ms (**1.91×**) | 1.745 → 0.898 ms (**1.94×**) |
+| Broadcast axis 0 | 1.121 → 0.527 ms (**2.14×**) | 1.124 → 0.767 ms (**1.47×**) | 1.777 → 0.908 ms (**1.96×**) | 1.765 → 0.889 ms (**2.01×**) |
+| Broadcast axis 1 | 1.136 → 0.273 ms (**4.17×**) | 1.118 → 0.291 ms (**3.80×**) | 0.725 → 0.563 ms (**1.30×**) | 0.717 → 0.542 ms (**1.32×**) |
+
+### Summary
+
+| Storage type | Median speedup across all 48 cells |
+|---|---:|
+| Integer | **3.00×** |
+| Double | **1.54×** |
+| Overall | **1.96×** |
+
+The complete grid is reproducible with:
+
+```sh
+RRAY_BENCH_ITERATIONS=25 Rscript bench/extremum.R
+```
+
+### Compiler verification
+
+Apple Clang 17 at `-O2` emitted **36 vectorized-loop reports**: both `na_rm` loop specializations for every one of the 18 generated `pmax`/`pmin` type-pair functions.
+
+- Double/double loops: vectorization width 2, interleave count 4.
+- All other native type pairings: vectorization width 4, interleave count 1 or 4.
+- The `*_one_*` helpers are scalar inline functions; these reports confirm that every helper use is inlined into a loop for which Clang emits a vectorized version.
+- Runtime stride and alias guards can still select the scalar fallback for non-vectorizable traversal shapes.
*** End Patch
